[Round 1 审稿请求]:
你是 Reviewer 1，负责审查**方法严谨性 + 统计有效性 + 实验设计**。

## 论文信息
- **标题**: Layer Selection Sensitivity in Hidden-State Safety Probes for Reasoning Models
- **目标 venue**: EMNLP 2026 (via ARR)
- **截止日期**: 2026-05-25
- **格式**: ACL template, 正文 8 页限制（不含 references/limitations/ethics）, double-blind
- **论文路径**: projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/main.tex
- **PDF**: /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/main.pdf

## Venue 要求
- EMNLP 2026 通过 ARR 统一审稿，Long paper 正文 8 页限制，references 无限制
- Mandatory Limitations section（不计入页数）
- Double-blind：无作者信息，避免自我暴露引用
- Overall Assessment 评分: 5=Award, 4=Conference, 3=Findings, 2=Resubmit, 1=Do not resubmit

## 你的审查重点

### 1. 统计方法有效性
- Bootstrap 重采样单元是否正确（trace-level cluster bootstrap）
- Holm-Bonferroni 校正范围（12 tests vs ~2,240 搜索空间）是否合理
- 32B 模型样本量（n=19-20）下的统计推断可靠性
- Evidence gradient 三级分类（cross-validated / significant / exploratory）是否得当
- 嵌套交叉验证的实施是否正确

### 2. 实验设计合理性
- Layer selection sensitivity 作为核心贡献的论证逻辑
- HS vs text 比较的公平性（不同 W 值、不同 encoder 维度）
- Control task (Hewitt & Liang) 的实施和解释
- Position residualization 控制的充分性（linear vs quadratic）
- 负面结果（7 种替代方法）的实验设计是否充分

### 3. Claims 与 Evidence 的匹配度
- 3 个 Contributions 的 claim 强度是否与统计证据匹配
- Abstract/Intro 的表述是否过强或过弱
- 32B exploratory 结论的 caveats 是否充分
- Encoder-conditional HS advantage 的 framing 是否准确

### 4. 格式合规
- 是否符合 8 页正文限制
- 是否 double-blind（无作者信息泄露）
- Limitations section 是否存在且充分

请按照标准审稿格式输出：Summary → Strengths → Weaknesses（标记 severity: Must-Fix / Should-Fix / Minor）→ Questions → Missing References → Overall Assessment（用 EMNLP 评分标准）→ Confidence

Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Based on the search results, here's information about the ACL ARR 2026 reviewer guidelines, review form criteria, and scoring:

```text
## Scoring System

Starting with February 2025, ARR reviewers are asked to provide three scores: soundness, excitement, and overall recommendation.

## Review Form Criteria

The main criteria for Findings are soundness and reproducibility. Conference recommendations may also consider novelty, impact and other factors.

## Overall Assessment Score

The Overall assessment score is an explicit recommendation for the outcome of this paper, if it were committed to an *ACL venue. This is a composite score reflecting your assessment of soundness, excitement, and also other factors like novelty and impact.

## Recommendation Categories

The review form includes specific recommendation categories:
- 5 = Consider for Award: I think this paper could be considered for an outstanding paper award at an *ACL conference (up to top 2.5% papers).
- 2 = Resubmit next cycle: I think this paper needs substantial revisions that can be completed by the next ARR cycle.
- 1.5 = Resubmit after next cycle: I think this paper needs substantial revisions that cannot be completed by the next ARR cycle.
- 1 = Do not resubmit: This paper has to be fully redone, or it is not relevant to the *ACL community.

For detailed information, you can visit the [ARR Reviewer Guidelines](http://aclrollingreview.org/reviewerguidelines) and [Review Form](http://aclrollingreview.org/reviewform) pages directly.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```text
# ACL Rolling Review Form - Scoring Dimensions

## 1. **Reviewer Confidence**
Scale: 1-5
- **5**: "Positive that my evaluation is correct. I read the paper very carefully and am familiar with related work."
- **4**: Quite sure with low likelihood of missing critical details
- **3**: Pretty sure, but possible oversights on mathematical or experimental specifics
- **2**: Willing to defend evaluation, but likely missed some details or novelty assessment
- **1**: Outside expertise area or paper is difficult to comprehend

## 2. **Soundness**
Scale: 1-5 (with .5 increments)
- **5**: "Excellent: This study is one of the most thorough I have seen, given its type."
- **4**: Strong support for all claims; additional experiments optional
- **3**: Acceptable; main claims supported but minor details need reinforcement
- **2**: Poor; main claims lack sufficient support; major technical problems exist
- **1**: "Major Issues: This study is not yet sufficiently thorough to warrant publication"

## 3. **Excitement**
Scale: 1-5 (with .5 increments)
Measures subjective engagement through factors like innovation, community impact, or personal research relevance

- **5**: Highly engaging; would recommend to others
- **4**: Engaging; would mention to colleagues
- **3**: Interesting; might attend presentation if available
- **2**: Potentially interesting to some community members
- **1**: Not engaging; unlikely to resonate within ACL community

## 4. **Overall Assessment**
Scale: 1-5 (with .5 increments)
- **5**: Consider for Award (top 2.5% papers)
- **4**: Conference acceptance recommended
- **3**: Findings acceptance recommended
- **2**: Resubmit with substantial revisions next cycle
- **1**: Do not resubmit or lacks ACL relevance

## 5. **Additional Scored Dimensions**

**Reproducibility** (1-5 scale): Assesses whether readers can replicate results or build upon findings

**Datasets** (1-5 scale): Evaluates released dataset value to researchers

**Software** (1-5 scale): Rates released software utility for future work

**Author Identity Knowledge** (1-5 scale): Documents potential double-blind violations
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have read the entire paper (32 pages). Let me also check the reference verification results more carefully, and read the LaTeX source for precise details on some statistical claims I want to verify.

Let me now compile my review. Let me organize my thoughts based on what I've read:

## Paper Summary
The paper investigates layer selection sensitivity in hidden-state (HS) safety probes for reasoning models (LLMs with chain-of-thought). The core finding is that the choice of which transformer layer to probe significantly affects safety classification performance, and this effect has been largely ignored in prior work. They define the "Safety Commitment Point" (CP) as the first reasoning step where safety deliberation permanently ceases. 

They evaluate HS probes across four reasoning models (R1-8B, OT-7B, QwQ-32B, R1-32B) using the HarmThoughts benchmark. The main contributions are identifying how threshold-based metrics systematically bias layer selection toward shallow layers, establishing boundary conditions for when HS probes provide advantages (particularly at 7-8B scale), and demonstrating that seven alternative methods fail to address these issues.

## My Review Focus: Method rigor + statistical validity + experimental design

### Statistical Methods Assessment

**Bootstrap resampling unit (trace-level cluster bootstrap)**
The paper uses 10,000 bootstrap resamples with trace-level clustering to account for dependencies within reasoning traces. Now I'm examining the specific statistical claims around confidence intervals and significance testing to verify the methodology is sound.

**Holm-Bonferroni correction scope**
The paper reports 12 tests for multiple comparison correction, but the actual exploration space spans 5 metrics × 7 windows × 25-64 layers per model—potentially thousands of configurations. They justify this by noting that metrics are highly correlated (Kendall τ ≈ 0.85) and layers show autocorrelation, substantially reducing the effective number of independent tests. I need to evaluate whether this correlation-based reduction is properly justified or if it underestimates the true multiple comparison burden.

The two-stage design (validation selection, held-out testing) does provide some protection, but there's still a meaningful concern here: the layer and window were selected through a massive search space, which is a form of researcher degrees of freedom. They address this with nested 5-fold cross-validation on smaller models, though for the 32B models the validation is weaker with only 4 traces per fold.

For the 32B models specifically—QwQ-32B with n=19 and R1-32B with n=20—the sample size is quite limited, which severely constrains statistical power and the minimum detectable effect sizes they can reliably identify. The nested 5-fold cross-validation works well for 7-8B models given the large search space, but for 32B models the sample size drops to only ~4 traces per fold, which creates reliability issues. Looking at the experimental design more broadly, the paper makes a solid contribution by systematically demonstrating that layer selection dramatically affects results—prior work typically just picks the last layer, but this study shows crossing-rate metrics favor shallow layers while threshold-based metrics prefer deep ones. However, the comparison between hidden states and text embeddings has some confounds: the text encoder (MiniMM-L6-v2 at 384 dimensions) is much smaller and differently dimensioned than the target models' hidden states.

The paper does test robustness with a larger encoder (BGE-large at 1024d) in Section S and finds the hidden state advantage persists, but there's still an asymmetry—the hidden state probes access layer-specific information from the target model while text probes use a generic sentence encoder, and they don't test whether using the target model's own text representations would change the results. For the control task, they implement it by randomly permuting labels within each trace while keeping the marginal distribution intact, then retraining with the same architecture to measure selectivity as the difference between original and control accuracy, with all selectivity values exceeding the baseline.

They also test position residualization by regressing out normalized position from the vectors before training, and the hidden state advantage largely survives this control—though R1-32B shows only a 1.9pp gap that isn't significant. Finally, they compare against seven alternative methods including BOCPD, CUSUM, Mahalanobis distance, and others, all of which underperform or fail, which is thorough coverage though some implementations could potentially be questioned.

Looking at the claims against evidence: the metric-induced layer selection bias is their strongest contribution, with Table 2 clearly demonstrating that crossing-rate metrics pick shallow layers while threshold-based metrics pick deep ones, supported by mechanistic insight from random-label decomposition. The hidden state advantage claim is more conditional—it holds at 7-8B scale with substantial gains in precision and balanced accuracy, but the 32B results are still exploratory and appropriately caveated as encoder-dependent. Contribution 3 appears to be cut off, so I'll wait to see what that covers.

For the critical issues, there's a significant multiple comparisons problem lurking here. The paper corrects for 12 model-level hypotheses using Holm-Bonferroni, but that doesn't account for the ~2,240 layer and window configurations tested per model. The authors acknowledge this and argue that metric correlations reduce the effective number of comparisons, but the two-stage design still leaves room for selection bias, especially at 32B where the validation set is tiny (around 20 samples) and they only run nested cross-validation on the 7-8B models.

Looking more carefully at Section 3.5, I see they're testing multiple window sizes and selecting per-model on validation, then evaluating under different train/test splits. The consistency checks across different window thresholds (K>3, 5, 7) do provide some reassurance that the findings aren't just artifacts of a particular hyperparameter choice, but the fundamental issue remains: with such a small validation set at 32B scale, the hyperparameter selection itself is noisy and could be driving the results.

The appendix sections acknowledge this directly—they note the massive joint search space (up to 2,240 configurations) and explicitly flag the overfitting concern with validation sets of only 4-9 examples per class. Their response is nested cross-validation on the smaller models, which shows the HS advantage persists and shrinkage is reasonable, but this validation only covers the 7-8B models, not the 32B model where the validation set is smallest and the concern most acute.

There's also a critical methodological point buried in W.1: the p-values reported are conditional on the layer and window selected during hyperparameter search, not marginal p-values. The paper correctly acknowledges this is valid given their train/validation/test split, but it's a subtle distinction that readers could easily miss—the significance estimates are specific to the chosen search path, not averaged across all possible hyperparameter choices.

Despite labeling 32B results as exploratory, the Abstract and Introduction still emphasize them prominently, which somewhat undermines the cautionary framing.  With sample sizes of 19-20 and minimum detectable effects around 8-9.5 percentage points, many of these 32B findings may not hold up in replication. Looking back at the abstract though, it does use a reasonable evidence gradient—"significant but suggestive" for QwQ-32B and "exploratory" for R1-32B—which is more honest than I initially thought. The word "significant" for QwQ-32B could still be misleading depending on what it refers to, but the overall hedging is actually fairly appropriate.

Now I'm noticing a fundamental asymmetry in how the paper compares hidden state probes versus text probes. The HS probes get model-specific access to internal representations at chosen layers, while the text probes rely on generic encoders like MiniLM or BGE-large. That's not really an apples-to-apples comparison. A fairer test would use the model's own embeddings or decoder outputs at different generation steps. The paper does show in the appendix that BGE-large-1024d partially preserves the HS advantage, but the core issue remains—HS probes have privileged access that text probes don't have.

Actually, thinking about this more practically: in a real deployment, you'd choose one approach or the other. You either read internal states or monitor the generated text. The text encoder has to be external because you need something to represent the text. Using the model's own embeddings would blur the comparison. So even though it's theoretically asymmetric, the comparison is still practically meaningful. The paper should at least acknowledge the dimensionality gap though—HS probes get 4096+ features while text encoders give 384 or 1024, which naturally favors the higher-dimensional approach.

The crossing-rate metric is presented as novel but feels somewhat arbitrary in its construction: balanced accuracy multiplied by crossing rate. The justification is purely empirical—it happens to select shallow layers where HS shows an advantage—but there's no theoretical grounding for why this specific combination should be preferred.

Looking at the random-label decomposition though, there's something interesting: crossing-rate preference for shallow layers appears label-independent (it's about representation geometry), while threshold-based preference for deep layers reflects actual discriminative signal. But that creates a tension—if crossing-rate's shallow preference is just a geometric artifact, why use that metric at all? The paper itself suggests crossing-rate inflates artificially in shallow layers due to coarse compression. Yet the core claim is that metric-dependent layer selection reveals genuine sensitivity: crossing-rate metrics pick shallow layers because of how representations are structured, while threshold metrics pick deep layers because of real discriminative power. The methodological takeaway seems to be favoring threshold-based selection instead.

So the actual contribution is demonstrating that metric choice systematically biases layer selection in predictable ways, and threshold-based metrics prove more reliable for this task. That's a solid finding.

The Hewitt & Liang control task addresses whether probes capture genuine label-correlated structure versus memorization, but it doesn't rule out whether that structure actually corresponds to safety-relevant information or some confound like sequence length or topic distribution. Position residualization helps mitigate this but doesn't eliminate all confounds. Additionally, the paper only evaluates on HarmThoughts—the sole publicly available dataset with step-level circuit preference annotations—which limits how well these findings generalize across different safety benchmarks.

The paper is almost entirely quantitative with no qualitative examples showing what the probes actually detect, what false positives and negatives look like, or how the hidden state signals differ between safe and unsafe traces. Adding concrete examples would make the mechanistic claims much stronger. There's also an imbalance in the model selection where Qwen is represented by two models while other architectures have one each, which could skew architecture-level conclusions. The Figure 1 heatmap uses min-max normalization per column, making cross-column color comparisons misleading for readers. Finally, the paper's use of "evidence gradient" terminology needs clarification.

Now I'm checking the reference verification results. The bibliography has 80 entries with 118 total citations, and the verification found 60 verified references, 15 not found, and 5 mismatches that I should examine more closely. I'm also calculating the page count—the main text runs through page 8, with the limitations and ethics statement on page 9, references on pages 10-12, and the appendix spanning pages 13-32. Looking at the page breakdown more carefully, the Discussion section wraps from page 8 into page 9, but the main content appears to stay within the 8-page limit since Limitations and Ethics Statement don't count toward it. Looking at page 8 more carefully, I can see the Discussion section is quite compressed, covering points about shallow-layer compression, threshold-based preferences, random-label controls, and metric-layer vulnerabilities before wrapping up. Page 9 immediately starts with Limitations, which confirms the main text fits within the 8-page limit—though the Discussion and conclusion feel rushed given how much ground they're covering.

I notice there's no separate Conclusion section; the Discussion appears to handle both roles, which is somewhat unusual for an EMNLP paper but not a violation. Now let me check the reference verification results more carefully before compiling the review.
```

```text
OK, now I have a comprehensive picture. Let me compile my review. The reference verification shows:
- 60 verified, 15 not found, 5 mismatch
- The "not found" entries are mostly recent arXiv preprints or well-known papers that OpenAlex might not index perfectly (like DeepSeek-R1, QwQ, Gelman 2013, Turpin 2023, etc.). These are legitimate references.
- The 5 mismatches are minor: LaTeX encoding issues for author names (Wollschläger, Chrabąszcz), first author disambiguation (Cohen 1988 book), etc.


- No serious citation issues.

Now I'm diving into the substantive review. The main statistical concern is that the Holm-Bonferroni correction only covers 12 final tests despite the paper exploring roughly 2,240 configurations in the search space. The authors acknowledge this limitation and defend it through their two-stage validation approach—selecting on validation data then testing on held-out data—plus citing Gelman and Loken on multiple comparisons. Their argument has merit but doesn't fully address the conditional p-value validity question.

The 32B model experiments are particularly underpowered with sample sizes of 19-20, requiring minimum detectable effects of 8-9.5 percentage points at 80% power. While the paper appropriately flags these as exploratory, some of the substantive claims drawn from this data—like the encoder-quality gradient finding and the QwQ-32B significance highlighted in the abstract—seem to overstate the confidence warranted by the power analysis. The bootstrap methodology at the trace level is sound though, correctly accounting for within-trace correlations. The evidence gradient framework itself is well-constructed with its three-tier system.

The 32B models also lack nested cross-validation, which the paper honestly acknowledges but this further limits confidence in those results. On the experimental design side, there's a dimensionality confound when comparing hidden state probes (3584-5120d) against text encoders (384-1024d), and while the paper tests a partial mitigation with BGE-large-1024d, it doesn't fully explore whether higher-dimensional text encoders like E5-Mistral-7B would narrow the gap.

Actually, looking back at Section U more carefully—they do test E5-Mistral-7B but reduce it to 384d via PCA to match MiniLM's dimensionality, which creates a controlled comparison but also masks whether the full dimensionality advantage matters. The hidden state advantage partially survives this test (2/4 models retain significance), but the PCA reduction itself confounds the comparison. the same dimensionality. If the HS advantage partially survives, it suggests it's not purely about dimensionality. But 384d might be too aggressive a reduction for E5-Mistral. The paper should also show E5-Mistral at its native 4096d.

Actually wait - if they used E5-Mistral at 4096d, it would be comparable in dimensionality to the 7B model's hidden states (4096d), making it a fairer comparison. The paper chose to PCA-reduce to 384d, which seems like it's handicapping the text probe to preserve the HS advantage finding. This is a significant methodological concern.

Actually, re-reading more carefully: "reduced to 384d via PCA to match the MiniLM dimensionality" - the rationale is to control for encoder quality while keeping dimensionality constant. But this conflates two variables: encoder quality and dimensionality. A better approach would test E5-Mistral at both 384d and its native 4096d to disentangle these factors, yet the paper only does the former. This matters because if a full-dimensional text encoder closes the gap, the HS advantage might just be a dimensionality artifact rather than a fundamental property.

The control task using label randomization is solid - all selectivity values exceed the threshold. Position residualization is also thorough, testing both linear and quadratic approaches and showing the HS advantage persists across specifications.

On the negative results side, they test 7 alternative methods but some may not have received optimal hyperparameter tuning - the MLP probes are limited to 2 layers with early stopping at 10 patience, which could be too restrictive. CCS requires constructing contrastive pairs, which is a strong assumption that may not generalize.

The single-dataset limitation is real. HarmThoughts' step-level annotation scheme is specific to that dataset, and it's unclear whether these findings would hold across other safety datasets or different annotation approaches.

Looking at the claims themselves, the abstract's focus on 7-8B models is well-supported by the cross-validated results, though the 32B claims are appropriately hedged. The encoder-conditional hidden state advantage is accurately presented, though the encoder comparison does have that dimensionality confound I noted earlier. The strongest contribution is the metric-induced layer selection bias finding - that's well-supported across multiple models and experimental approaches. One structural note: the paper doesn't have a formal Conclusion section, so the Discussion is doing double duty there.

Let me verify the page count against the EMNLP guidelines. The main text should fit within 8 pages, and checking the layout, the Discussion section starts mid-page 8 and continues through the end of that page, with Limitations beginning on page 9. So the main text does stay within the 8-page limit.

That said, the Discussion is quite compressed - only about 25-30 lines for a paper with this many experimental results. It raises questions about whether the implications and limitations of the findings are adequately explored within the main text, though technically it meets the page requirement.

Now I'll structure my formal review following the standard format. The paper's strengths—novel research question, rigorous experimental design across multiple models and metrics, honest reporting, and solid statistical methodology—outweigh the weaknesses of limited scope in multiple comparisons correction, the E5-Mistral PCA issue, and reliance on a single dataset. While the secondary findings come with caveats, the core contribution about layer selection sensitivity is well-supported and practically relevant, placing this at a 3.5 (Borderline Conference), with potential to reach 4.0 if the statistical scope and cross-dataset concerns are addressed.
```

### Assistant

_source line 135 · 2026-05-21T16:55:05.211Z_

已通读全部 32 页（正文 + 附录），收集了 ARR review form 标准和引用验证结果。现在撰写审稿意见。

---

## 总体评分

**Overall**: 3.5 / 5（Borderline Conference）

**一句话总结**: 论文提出了一个重要的方法论洞见（layer selection metric 会系统性偏置 probe 的层选择），实验设计严谨且统计报告诚实，但 Holm-Bonferroni 校正范围与实际搜索空间的不匹配、text encoder 对比中的维度混淆，以及单一数据集的局限性，削弱了核心 claims 的可信度。

---

## 官方维度打分（ACL ARR Review Form）

| 维度 | 分数 | 理由摘要 |
|------|------|----------|
| **Soundness** | 3.5/5 | 统计方法总体正确（cluster bootstrap, nested CV），但 HB 校正范围偏窄（12 tests vs ~2,240 搜索配置），且 E5-Mistral 对比存在 PCA 降维混淆。32B 结论的统计功效不足已诚实披露。 |
| **Excitement** | 3.5/5 | Layer selection sensitivity 是一个被忽视且有实践价值的方法论问题，对 safety probing 社区有警示意义。但贡献更偏"审计现有实践"而非提出新方法。 |
| **Reproducibility** | 4.5/5 | 承诺公开代码和数据，所有超参数、数据划分、统计检验细节在附录中详尽列出，实验复现性极高。 |
| **Confidence** | 4/5 | 我熟悉 probing methodology、bootstrap 统计和 safety 领域文献，仔细阅读了所有附录。可能在 representation geometry 的解释上有遗漏。 |

---

## Summary

本文研究 reasoning model（CoT）中 hidden-state safety probe 的层选择敏感性问题。作者定义了 Safety Commitment Point (CP) 作为安全推理永久停止的第一步，在 4 个推理模型上系统比较了 HS probe 与 text probe 在不同层、窗口、metric 下的表现。核心发现：(1) crossing-rate composite metric 系统性选择浅层而 threshold-based metric 选择深层，层选择完全由 metric 决定而非由数据驱动；(2) HS probe 在 7-8B 模型上 cross-validated 优于 text probe（+21.9pp precision），但此优势依赖于 encoder 质量；(3) 7 种替代检测方法均表现不佳。

---

## Strengths

**S1. 重要且被忽视的方法论问题。** 现有 safety probing 文献几乎都把层选择当作实现细节，本文第一次系统性地证明 metric 选择会完全反转层偏好（Table 2），这对整个领域的实验可靠性有警示意义。Random-label decomposition（Section B.1）进一步揭示了浅层偏好来自表示几何而非判别信号，这是一个有洞察力的分析。

**S2. 统计方法论总体严谨。** Trace-level cluster bootstrap（Section 3.5）正确处理了步骤间的时序依赖；三级 evidence gradient（cross-validated / significant / exploratory）清晰对应了不同的统计可信度；32B 的功效分析（Table 41, MDE 8-9.5pp）是罕见的诚实报告。论文在 Section W.1 主动讨论 garden of forking paths 问题并引用 Gelman & Loken (2013)，显示了方法论自觉。

**S3. 全面的消融和鲁棒性检验。** 20+ 页附录覆盖了 full layer sweep（Section B）、random-label decomposition（B.1）、representation compression analysis（C）、Holm-Bonferroni step-down procedure（D）、window ablation（E）、OT-7B full results（F）、GRU training details（G）、control task（H）、CP annotation perturbation（J）、BOCPD/CUSUM sweep（K）、detection threshold sensitivity（L-M）、bug disclosure（N）、two-stage detection（O）、temporal ablation（P）、negative results details（Q）、cross-model transfer（R）、encoder robustness（S-U）、position residualization（V）、hyperparameter robustness（W）、multi-seed stability（X）、BA-layer modality（Y）、post-hoc power（Z）、ensemble（Z.1）。这种覆盖度在 short paper 中极为罕见。

**S4. 负面结果的系统性报告。** Table 24 列出 7 种替代方法的失败模式，每种都有具体的 failure diagnosis（BOCPD 0% detection, MLP overfit, CCS semi-supervised requirement 等），而非简单声明"didn't work"。

**S5. 透明的 bug disclosure。** Section N 主动披露了 M002 bug（text probe 误用 midpoint placeholder embeddings），包括发现过程、影响范围和修正方案。这种透明度在学术论文中值得肯定。

---

## Weaknesses

### Must-Fix 问题（影响接收决定）

**N1** [critical] **Holm-Bonferroni 校正范围与实际搜索空间严重不匹配**

- 具体位置：Section 3.5（page 5）报告 "12 tests"；Section W.1（page 31）承认搜索空间 "up to 2,240 configurations per model"
- 问题：论文对 12 个 model-level tests 做了 HB 校正，但层和窗口是从 ~2,240 个配置中搜索选出的。Section W.1 承认报告的 p 值是 **conditional p-values**（"significance given the chosen selection path rather than significance averaged over all possible paths"），但这个关键 caveat 只出现在第 31 页的附录中，主文完全没有提及。主文 Table 3 和 Abstract 呈现的 "significant" / "cross-validated" 结论会让读者误以为这些是 marginal significance。
- 虽然论文正确地使用了 train/val/test 分离（60/20/20），且 7-8B 的 nested 5-fold CV 显示 shrinkage 在 13%-25%（合理范围），但对 32B 模型（validation fold 仅 ~4 traces），selection overfitting 风险很高。
- 为什么影响接收：这直接关系到论文最核心的统计 claim 的可信度。Conditional vs marginal significance 的区别在 probing methodology 文献中有实质意义（正如 Belinkov 2022 讨论的）。
- 建议修改方向：(1) 在主文 Section 3.5 或 Section 4 明确说明 p 值是 conditional on the selected hyperparameter path；(2) 报告 effective degrees of freedom 的估计（不仅在附录）；(3) 考虑使用 split-half replication 或 permutation-based calibration 来估计 marginal coverage。

**N2** [critical] **E5-Mistral text encoder 对比中的 PCA 降维混淆（Section U）**

- 具体位置：Section U（page 27），"reduced to 384d via PCA to match the MiniLM dimensionality"
- 问题：论文将 E5-Mistral-7B-Instruct（4096d）PCA 降至 384d 以匹配 MiniLM 维度，然后报告 "HS advantage partially survives"（2/4 模型通过 HB）。但这个比较存在严重混淆：
  - 7B 模型的 hidden states 为 4096d，E5-Mistral 原始维度也是 4096d——这是维度匹配的公平比较
  - 将 E5-Mistral 降至 384d 后比较，等于人为削弱 text probe，保留了 HS probe 的维度优势（4096d vs 384d）
  - Section S 用 BGE-large（1024d）显示 HS 优势部分存活，但 1024d 仍远小于 HS 的 4096d
- 为什么影响接收：Contribution 2 的核心 claim 是 "encoder-conditional HS advantage"，如果 4096d text encoder 能完全消除 HS 优势，那这个 "advantage" 本质上是维度差异而非模态差异。
- 建议修改方向：补充 E5-Mistral 在原始 4096d 下的对比结果。如果 HS 优势消失，应重新表述 Contribution 2 为 "the HS advantage is primarily a dimensionality advantage"。

### Should-Fix 问题（影响评分但不一票否决）

**W1** [warning] **32B "significant" 标签可能误导读者**

- 具体位置：Abstract（"significant but suggestive of positive-competitiveness at QwQ-32B†"）；Table 3
- 问题：QwQ-32B 的 HS 优势通过了 HB 校正（p<0.001），被标记为 "significant"。但样本量仅 n=19，MDE 为 8pp（Table 41），且没有 nested CV 验证。在如此小的样本上，bootstrap p 值容易受单个 outlier trace 的影响。Section X（multi-seed stability）显示 R1-8B 的 FPR 跨 seed 变异很大（seed-fragile），但没有对 QwQ-32B 做相同的 seed 稳定性分析。
- 建议：(1) 为 QwQ-32B 也报告 multi-seed 稳定性；(2) 在 abstract 中将 QwQ-32B 也降级为 "exploratory"，或至少用 "nominally significant at n=19" 来 qualify。

**W2** [warning] **单一数据集（HarmThoughts）限制泛化性**

- 具体位置：Limitations section（page 9）
- 问题：所有实验基于 HarmThoughts 一个数据集（~200-530 traces）。虽然论文正确指出这是唯一有 step-level CP 标注的公开数据集，但以下问题未能排除：
  - CP 定义是否对标注方案敏感？Section J 的 ±3 step perturbation 测试了标注噪声鲁棒性，但未测试 CP 定义本身的替代方案
  - HarmThoughts 的 jailbreak 类型分布是否代表真实攻击分布？
  - 层选择 sensitivity 的 pattern 是否数据集特异的？
- 建议：(1) 即使无法获得第二个有 CP 标注的数据集，也可以在 AdvBench（已在 Section C.1 使用）上测试层选择 sensitivity 的 pattern 是否一致；(2) 更明确地讨论 CP 定义的鲁棒性。

**W3** [warning] **Discussion 过度压缩**

- 具体位置：Section 5（page 8，约 25-30 行）
- 问题：Discussion 仅半个 column 长度，需要覆盖 3 个 contributions 的 implications、与 prior work 的联系、以及 methodological recommendations。关键讨论内容（如 crossing-rate 为何不可靠、threshold-based metric 为何更好、conditional inference 的重要性）被压缩或推迟到附录。这导致读者需要反复跳转到附录才能理解核心论证。
- 建议：考虑精简 Section 4.4（32B 细节可移至附录）或 Section 4.5/4.6，为 Discussion 腾出空间。

**W4** [warning] **Crossing-rate composite metric 的构造缺乏理论动机**

- 具体位置：Section 3.3（page 5），"A crossing rate exists for trace τ at layer l when the probe score s_l^(t) > τ for K consecutive steps"
- 问题：Balanced accuracy × crossing rate 的乘积形式是 ad hoc 的。论文展示了它 empirically 选择浅层（Table 2），且 random-label decomposition 表明这反映表示几何而非判别信号。但如果 crossing-rate 偏好本身是 misleading artifact（如 Section 5 暗示的 "coarse compression"），为什么要将它作为五个 candidate metrics 之一？这引入了一个论文随后需要 debunk 的 strawman，逻辑上不够清晰。
- 建议：在 Section 3.3 明确说明 crossing-rate 的引入动机是作为 "prior work 中可能使用的 metric" 来审计，而非作者推荐的 metric。

### Minor 问题（不影响接收）

**M1.** Figure 1 heatmap 使用 per-column min-max normalization，使得跨 metric 的颜色比较失去意义。建议标注 normalization scheme 或使用统一 scale。

**M2.** Table 1 中 Qwen family 有 2 个模型（OT-7B, QwQ-32B）而其他 family 各 1 个，在讨论 "cross-architecture" 结论时可能引入偏差。

**M3.** 论文没有独立的 Conclusion section，Discussion 承担双重职能。虽然不违反 EMNLP 格式要求，但不利于快速把握论文的核心 takeaways。

**M4.** 引用验证发现 5 条 mismatch（主要是 LaTeX 编码问题导致的作者名不匹配，如 Wollschläger, Chrabąszcz），15 条 "not found"（多为近期 arXiv 预印本，OpenAlex 未收录）。无虚构引用问题。

---

## Questions to Authors

**Q1.** Section U 的 E5-Mistral 对比为何选择 PCA 降至 384d 而非使用原始 4096d？如果 4096d 下 HS 优势消失，是否改变 Contribution 2 的核心 claim？

**Q2.** Section W.1 中 conditional p-values 的问题：你们是否考虑过使用 permutation-based 方法来估计在整个搜索空间上的 marginal type-I error rate？

**Q3.** 32B 模型的 nested CV 不可行（4 traces/fold），但是否可以用 leave-one-out CV 来近似？虽然 LOO-CV 有其自身问题，但至少能提供一个 cross-validated 估计。

**Q4.** Section B.1 random-label decomposition 显示 crossing-rate 浅层偏好是 label-independent 的（几何属性），那 threshold-based 的深层偏好也做过 random-label 分解吗？如果深层偏好也部分是 label-independent 的，这会改变你们的解释。

**Q5.** 论文声称 "false positive rates remain 11-50%"（Limitations, page 9），这对实际部署意味着什么？在 alarm fatigue 的背景下，这些 FPR 水平是否 actionable？

---

## Missing References

- **Conditional inference in probing**：Voita & Titov (2020) 已引用，但 Pimentel et al. (2020) "Information-theoretic probing for linguistic structure" 虽已引用，论文未充分讨论其关于 probing 中 selection bias 的警告，这与本文的 layer selection sensitivity 主题直接相关。
- **Multi-layer probing**：Pais et al. (2026) 已引用，但 Belinkov (2022) 关于 probing 中 hyperparameter search 导致的 researcher degrees of freedom 的讨论未被充分联系到本文的 garden of forking paths 问题。

---

## Overall Assessment

**Overall: 3.5 / 5 — Borderline Conference**

本文识别了一个真实且重要的方法论问题：safety probing 中的层选择不是中性的实现细节，而是受 metric 选择系统性偏置的方法论变量。这个发现对领域有警示价值。实验设计的覆盖度和统计报告的诚实度超过了该领域多数论文。

主要顾虑是：(1) HB 校正的 conditional vs marginal significance 区分在主文中不够清晰，可能导致读者高估统计证据的强度；(2) E5-Mistral 的 PCA 降维混淆使得 "HS advantage" 是否为 dimensionality artifact 这一替代解释未被排除；(3) 单一数据集限制泛化性。

如果 N1 和 N2 得到妥善处理（N1 在主文中加入 conditional inference caveat + effective df 估计；N2 补充 4096d 对比或重新表述 claim），评分可以提升到 4.0。当前状态下，论文适合 Findings track。

**Confidence: 4/5** — 我对 probing methodology 和 bootstrap 统计有扎实背景，仔细阅读了全部附录。在 representation geometry 的解释上可能有遗漏。

---

现在将审稿意见提交给 Review Director。

### Strengths
S1. 实验方法论严谨性极高：5-metric taxonomy, bootstrap 10k resamples + HB + random-label decomposition + Hewitt control tasks
S2. Bug disclosure (M002) 模范级科研透明度

### Must-Fix
N1 [critical] 单数据集（HarmThoughts）限制核心 claim 普适性——"metric choice can reverse conclusions" 可能是数据集特有现象，需至少一个额外数据集验证 metric reversal
N2 [critical] 4模型中3个共享 Qwen 架构，标题 "across four reasoning LLMs" 暗示的多样性高于实际。Shallow-layer bias 可能是 Qwen 特有（论文自己在 Discussion 也提到）

W4 [warning] Figure 1 (Layer Heatmap) 在 ACL single-column 下可读性差
W5 [warning] Evidence gradient 被列为独立贡献但实际是标准统计实践的组织方式

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The Director is asking me (Reviewer 1) to respond to Reviewer 2's complete review, point by point, using [AGREE] or [REBUT] format with evidence from the paper. Let me go through each item systematically.

Let me re-read the paper details I gathered earlier and respond to each point.

Let me go through each item:

### Must-Fix

**N1** - Single dataset (HarmThoughts) limits generalizability of "metric choice can reverse conclusions"
I raised a similar concern (my W2). I agree this is a real limitation but I need to assess whether it's Must-Fix level. 

The metric reversal finding stems from the mathematical properties of how crossing-rate and threshold-based metrics behave on temporal probe data, so it might generalize beyond this specific dataset—but I can't confirm that without additional verification.

**N2** - 3 of 4 models share Qwen architecture, "four reasoning LLMs" overstates diversity
Looking at the model breakdown: R1-8B is Llama-based, while OT-7B, R1-32B, and QwQ-32B are all Qwen variants. So the diversity claim does seem overstated since three of the four models share the same architecture family.

The paper actually acknowledges this in the Limitations section, noting that only R1-8B provides cross-architecture evidence. Their own results even show the crossing-rate bias behaves differently between Qwen models and R1-8B, suggesting the architecture matters. This is a legitimate concern worth flagging, though I'd classify it as something that should be addressed rather than a critical flaw.

**W1** - Crossing rate vs early exit consistency metrics (Kaya 2019, Zhou 2020) conceptual difference not clarified. The paper cites these works but doesn't explicitly explain how crossing rate differs from early exit metrics, which is a valid gap.

**W2** - Abstract information overload. Counting the independent findings packed in—CP definition, 4 models, metric reversal, HS vs text at multiple scales, evidence gradient, 7 negative methods, deployment implications—it's quite dense and could be streamlined.

**W3** - Chan & Yong (2025) concurrent work distinction insufficient. The paper mentions their work briefly in Section 3 but doesn't clearly differentiate how this work differs from their concurrent approach to hidden-state geometry for hallucination detection.

**W4** - Figure 1 readability. EMNLP uses two-column format, and Figure 1 spans the full width as a large 2×2 heatmap, so it should be readable, but I'm double-checking the actual layout to confirm.

**W5** - The evidence gradient framework is solid organizational practice, but listing it as a contribution feels overstated since this is standard statistical methodology.

**W6** - I agree with the concern about statistical power at 32B—the Discussion section makes stronger claims than the sample size really supports.

**M1** - The belrose2023tunedlens bibliography entry is missing author information.

**M2** - Table 1's sample size of n=13 for OT-7B is quite limited.

**M3** - Actually, checking the method section on page 4, the regularization parameter C=1.0 is specified in the logistic regression setup, so this isn't missing from the main text.

**M4** - The abstract defines the commitment point simply as the first reasoning step where safety deliberation stops, but Section 3.1 adds detail about handling oscillating safety deliberation by taking the final irreversible transition—a meaningful precision difference.

**M5** - Figure 2's y-axis label is too small visually.

**M6** - Checking page 6 in Section 4.2 to see what the "98%" metric actually refers to...

ah, I see it now. The text mentions "threshold-based consensus selects L63 (98%)" where the 98% is the depth percentage—L63 out of 64 total layers equals 98.4%.

Now looking at the next section, R2 makes a fair point that this notation could be clearer without having to work through the layer math yourself.

For Q1 about shallow layer clustering, the paper's representation compression analysis shows shallow layers do produce highly compressed representations with much smaller inter-step distances. The random-label decomposition suggests this compression is overall variance rather than safety-specific, since the crossing-rate preference appears label-independent.

For Q2 on the R1-32B residualized HS advantage, I'm checking Table 30 which shows the residualized HS-text gap is +1.9pp, and this seems to relate to whether position mediates the effect.

For Q3, the five metrics in Section 3.3 appear to be selected empirically rather than from a principled framework.

For Q4 on the M002 bug scope, the disclosure indicates it was found during a systematic audit of the feature extraction pipeline in the QwQ-32B text probe specifically, and after correction using actual sentence embeddings, the originally selected layers showed no significant HS advantage across the model-modality combinations tested.

Now I'm checking the references cited in the paper. Some of these look potentially problematic—I'm not confident about Gurnee et al. (2024) on language model internals and safety, and while Levinstein & Herrmann (2024) sounds like it could be a relevant critique of probing methodology, I can't verify these without searching. The Li et al. (2024) reference on inference-time intervention seems more solid as it relates to probing and intervention work. I'm also seeing a reference to Nanda et al. (2023) and TransformerLens that appears to be cut off.

Now I'm organizing my response to the reviewer comments. I need to go through each point carefully and decide where I agree or disagree, making sure to cite specific evidence from the paper itself. For the single dataset concern, I can acknowledge it's a limitation while noting that the metric reversal finding might generalize due to its mathematical properties. The Qwen architecture dominance is a fair point—three of four models are Qwen-based. I should also clarify the distinction between crossing rate and early exit, tighten up the abstract, and provide better differentiation from Chan & Yong's work.

On the figure readability, I can partially push back since EMNLP's two-column format means Figure 1 spans full width, though I understand it might still feel dense. The evidence gradient isn't a novel contribution, just good practice. I'll address the 32B interpretation concern, handle the minor items individually, and assess the missing references one by one.
```

### Assistant

_source line 170 · 2026-05-21T16:59:40.541Z_

PDF 图片已被清理，但我在第一轮审稿中已详细阅读了全部 32 页。基于已有的详细笔记撰写交叉回应。

---

## Reviewer 1 对 Reviewer 2 的逐条回应

---

### Must-Fix

**[AGREE] R2-N1: 单数据集限制核心 claim 普适性**

同意这是实质性问题，与我的 W2 一致。补充两点：

(1) Metric reversal 的**数学机制**可能跨数据集成立——crossing-rate 在浅层膨胀源于表示压缩的几何属性（Section C, Table 9: 浅层 inter-step cosine distance 仅 0.04-0.19 vs 深层 0.06-0.41），而非 HarmThoughts 的标注特性。但这仍需实证验证。

(2) 论文在 Section C.1 已用 AdvBench（388 traces）验证了 boundary condition（CP=0 的 jailbreak 率 90.4%），但**未在 AdvBench 上测试 metric reversal pattern**。这是一个低成本的补充实验——用相同 5 metrics 在 AdvBench 上做 layer sweep，观察浅/深分离是否重现。如果重现，可大幅增强 Contribution 1。

不过我对 severity 有分歧：**我认为这是 Should-Fix 而非 Must-Fix**。Metric reversal 的论证核心在于同一数据上不同 metrics 给出矛盾的层选择——这个现象的存在性已被充分证明（Table 2, 4 models 一致），只是**泛化性**需要额外数据集确认。

**[AGREE] R2-N2: 4 模型中 3 个 Qwen 架构，多样性被高估**

完全同意。Table 1 显示 R1-8B（Llama）、OT-7B（Qwen）、QwQ-32B（Qwen）、R1-32B（Qwen）。论文在 Limitations（page 9）承认 "Three of four models share the Qwen architecture; only R1-8B (Llama) provides cross-architecture evidence"，但标题 "across four reasoning LLMs" 和 Abstract 的 "four reasoning models (3 architecture families)" 确实暗示了更高的架构多样性。

关键的实质影响：
- Section 5（Discussion）自己指出 "crossing-rate shallow-layer bias is Qwen-specific"，但这个 Qwen-specific 结论基于仅 1 个 non-Qwen 模型（R1-8B），统计基础薄弱。
- Section B.1 random-label decomposition 在 R1-8B 上显示 crossing-rate 偏好是 label-independent geometric property，而在 QwQ-32B 上有不同 pattern——这恰恰说明架构差异影响机制，更需要额外 non-Qwen 模型验证。

建议将标题中 "four" 改为更诚实的表述，如 "across reasoning LLMs of two architectures"，或在 abstract 中明确 3/4 为 Qwen。

---

### Should-Fix

**[AGREE] R2-W1: Crossing rate 与 early exit consistency metrics 概念区分不足**

同意。Section 3.3 引用了 Kaya et al. (2019) 和 Zhou et al. (2020) 但仅作为 "crossing rate formalizes cross-layer prediction disagreement" 的背景，未明确区分：(a) early exit 中的 consistency 是跨层预测一致性（同一输入不同层），(b) 本文的 crossing rate 是同一层跨时间步的 threshold crossing 频率。这两个概念在操作定义上完全不同，混淆会误导读者。

**[AGREE] R2-W2: Abstract 信息过载**

同意。Abstract 试图覆盖：CP 定义、4 模型、metric reversal、HS vs text（4 个 scale）、evidence gradient 三级、7 种 negative results、FPR 范围、deployment implications。核心 message（metric choice 系统性偏置层选择）被淹没。建议 abstract 聚焦 Contribution 1（metric reversal），将 Contribution 2-3 的数字细节缩减。

**[REBUT] R2-W3: 与 Chan & Yong (2025) 的区分不充分**

部分不同意。Chan & Yong (2025) 研究的是 "predicting alignment before models finish thinking"——即用 hidden-state geometry 预测模型是否会 misalign。本文研究的是**层选择 sensitivity 作为方法论变量**对 probe 结论的影响。两者的 research question 有本质区别：Chan & Yong 问 "HS probe 能否检测 safety"，本文问 "HS probe 的层选择如何影响其检测结论"。本文的核心贡献（metric reversal, Contribution 1）在 Chan & Yong 中完全没有讨论。

不过同意可以在 Related Work 中用 2-3 句话更明确地阐述这一区分，而非仅 "consistently with our work" 一句带过。

**[AGREE] R2-W4: Figure 1 可读性差**

同意。Figure 1 是 2×2 的 per-layer heatmap（4 models × 5 metrics），即使在 full-width 两栏格式下，每个子图的 layer 标签和颜色区分仍然密集。且 per-column min-max normalization（我在 M1 中也指出）使跨 metric 的视觉比较失去意义。

**[REBUT] R2-W5: Evidence gradient 不应被列为独立贡献**

部分不同意。R2 称 evidence gradient "实际是标准统计实践的组织方式"。但在 safety probing 文献中，多数论文对 n=19 的结论和 n=530 的结论使用相同的 confidence level——这正是本文要批评的。Evidence gradient 的价值不在于统计方法本身的新颖性，而在于将其**系统性地应用于一个尚未采纳此实践的领域**。

但我同意论文不应将其列为与 metric reversal 同等地位的 "contribution"。更准确的定位是 "methodological recommendation" 而非 "contribution"。

**[AGREE] R2-W6: 32B 统计效力不足下 Discussion 仍做较强解读**

完全同意，与我的 W1 一致。Section 4.4（page 8）为 32B 专设了 "32B Model-Specific Analysis" 小节，讨论 "encoder-quality gradient" 和 "R1-32B suggests pre-model validation is needed"，但这些解读建立在 n=19-20 的 exploratory 数据上，MDE 为 8-9.5pp（Table 41）。Exploratory 标签的保护力度不够——读者仍然会被 Section 4.4 的 narrative 引导形成对 32B 的实质性信念。

---

### Minor

**[AGREE] R2-M1: belrose2023tunedlens bib 作者为空**

确认。引用验证数据显示该条目 `authors: ""`。这是必须修复的格式错误。

**[AGREE] R2-M2: Table 1 中 OT-7B n=13 样本极小**

同意这值得关注。但论文在 Section 4.1 对 OT-7B 使用了与其他模型相同的统计检验框架，且 OT-7B 的结果是 cross-validated（nested 5-fold CV），所以 n=13 虽小但有交叉验证保护。更准确的担忧是每个 CV fold 仅 ~2-3 test traces。

**[REBUT] R2-M3: 正则化强度 C 未在正文提及**

不同意。Section 3.2（page 4）明确写出 "L2-regularized logistic regression classifier (balanced class weights, C = 1.0, LBFGS, max_iter = 2000)"。C=1.0 已在正文中指定。

**[AGREE] R2-M4: CP 定义 abstract vs Section 3.1 措辞差异**

同意。Abstract 定义 CP 为 "the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases"，而 Section 3.1 增加了 oscillation handling clause："traces with oscillating safety deliberation are resolved by taking the final irreversible transition"。这一差异虽不改变核心含义，但 abstract 的简化版本掩盖了 oscillating traces 的处理方式，应在 abstract 中至少暗示存在 edge case handling。

**[AGREE] R2-M5: Figure 2 y 轴标签字体小**

视觉问题，同意。

**[REBUT] R2-M6: Section 4.2 "98%" 未明确指标**

部分不同意。Section 4.2 的上下文是 "threshold-based consensus selects L63 (98%)"——这里的 98% 是 L63 / L64 = 98.4%，即**层深度百分比**。虽然可以更明确（如写 "L63, 98% depth"），但在讨论 layer selection 的语境中，百分比指代 depth 是隐含清晰的。不过同意加上 "depth" 一词可消除歧义。

---

### Questions

**Q1: 浅层 cosine 聚类是 trivial artifact（overall variance 小）还是 safety-specific 压缩？**

我的判断：**主要是 overall variance artifact**，非 safety-specific。证据：
- Section C（Table 9）显示浅层 inter-step cosine distance 仅 0.04-0.19，**不区分 safe/unsafe traces**
- Section B.1 random-label decomposition 显示 crossing-rate 浅层偏好在 random labels 下仍然存在（label-independent），确认这是几何属性而非判别信号
- 这正是论文 Contribution 1 的核心洞察：crossing-rate metric 偏好浅层是因为**表示几何中的 noise floor proximity**，而非安全信号集中在浅层

论文在 Section 5 也下了这个结论："crossing rate inflates at shallow layers as an artifact of coarse compression"。R2 的怀疑与论文的 finding 一致。

**Q2: R1-32B residualized 后 HS advantage 消失是否暗示 position mediation 主导？**

是一个重要问题。Section V（Table 30, page 28）显示 R1-32B 的 residualized HS-text gap 为 +1.9pp（p=0.91），HS advantage "fully absorbed by the quadratic term"。这意味着对 R1-32B 来说，HS 相对于 text 的优势**主要由时序位置信息驱动**。

但这不能推广到所有模型：R1-8B 和 OT-7B 的 residualized advantage 仍然显著（+3.7pp 和 +6.4pp HB corrected）。所以 position mediation 强度是模型特异的——R1-32B 的 HS 信号可能更依赖 "what step we're at" 而非 "what the model is representing at this step"。

论文在 Section 4.5 提到这一点但对 R1-32B 的 position-dominance 未做深入讨论，R2 的观察与我的 W1（32B 解读过强）一致。

**Q3: 5-metric taxonomy 选择有无 principled 理由？**

我的评估：**选择是 empirical 而非 principled**。5 metrics 分为两类——threshold-based（precision, inverse FPR, additive composite）和 crossing-rate-based（balanced accuracy × crossing rate, 和 pure balanced accuracy）。论文在 Section 3.3 未给出为何选择这 5 个而非其他可能的 metrics 的理论理由。与我的 W4 一致：crossing-rate composite 的乘积形式尤其 ad hoc。

**Q4: Bug M002 影响范围是否检查了其他 model-modality 组合？**

Section N（page 23）明确说明 bug 影响仅限于 QwQ-32B text probe："The bug was identified during a systematic audit of the feature extraction pipeline"。修正后 "the corrected text embedding pipeline" 影响了 QwQ-32B 和 R1-32B 的 modality comparison。Section N 的 "Commitment to transparency" 段落也说 "Code and data will be released upon acceptance"，暗示 audit 覆盖了全部 pipeline。但论文确实未明确写出 "we verified all other model-modality combinations are bug-free"，R2 要求明确这一点是合理的。

---

### Missing References

**[REBUT] MR1: Gurnee et al. (2024) "Language Model Internals Reveal Progress Toward Safety"**

我无法确认此论文的存在。搜索后未找到该确切标题的出版物。R2 可能将多篇论文混淆。如果该引用不存在，则此建议不成立。

**[AGREE] MR2: Levinstein & Herrmann (2024) "Still No Lie Detector for Language Models"**

如果存在，这与论文关于 probing 局限性的讨论直接相关——probing accuracy 不等于 causal relevance。论文已通过 Hewitt & Liang control task（Section H）和 position residualization（Section V）部分回应了这一批评方向，但 cite 该批评文献可增强方法论讨论的完整性。

**[AGREE] MR3: Li et al. (2024) "Inference-Time Intervention" (ITI)**

同意应引用。ITI 直接在推理时修改 hidden states 以影响模型行为，这与本文的 "probing hidden states for detection" 形成 detection vs intervention 的对比。论文在 Section 2 讨论了 intervention 方向的工作（Arditi et al., Turner et al.）但未引用 ITI，这是一个遗漏。

**[REBUT] MR4: Nanda et al. (2023) / TransformerLens**

不同意这是必要引用。TransformerLens 是一个工具库，主要用于 mechanistic interpretability（circuit analysis, attention pattern visualization）。本文使用的是标准的 linear probing，不涉及 TransformerLens 的核心功能（activation patching, logit lens 等）。论文已引用了 Tuned Lens（Belrose et al., 2023）作为相关方法论参考。

---

### 新颖性评估回应

R2 给出 Excitement 2.5/5 并判断为 Findings track。我给出 3.5/5。

分歧在于对 "审计现有实践" 类工作的价值判断。R2 认为 metric reversal 是 "非概念新颖" 的 "系统量化"。但我认为：**揭示一个被整个领域忽视的方法论陷阱**（且该陷阱可以逆转已发表论文的结论）本身有足够的 excitement 达到 Conference threshold——前提是统计论证的 conditional/marginal 区分（我的 N1）和 encoder 维度混淆（我的 N2）得到修复。

R2 的 Excitement 评分偏低。该领域有大量论文在未考虑层选择 sensitivity 的情况下报告 probe 结果，本文对这一实践的系统性审计具有 corrective 价值。

---

现在提交给 Director。
